People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I used to do things all the time—hiking, coaching, playing guitar, calling people back. Now I don’t really do any of it.

I sit in my little rental place for hours sometimes, knowing I should get up, clean, make a call, whatever, but I just… can’t start. It’s like the engine won’t turn over. I don’t know. I used to be dependable, you know? Protective services, schedules, radios, checklists—everything had a place. Now even answering a text feels like moving a couch.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really feel like myself anymore.

I used to hike a lot, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—nothing professional, just sitting around with it—but I don’t really do any of that now. Even calling people back, I just… don’t. I’ll see the phone and think I should, and then I’ll put it down, or I’ll start looking at something else, like the crack near the window in my rental, and then hours go by.

Some days I’m just sitting there knowing there are things I’m supposed to do, things I used to want to do, and I can’t get myself started. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost interest in most of it.

But no, I haven’t noticed some big weight drop or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, maybe.\n\nI’ll think I should’ve done something, or I’ll remember some little thing from work or from years ago, and it kind of sticks for a bit. But then it turns into something else, like I’ll start thinking about whether I locked a gate or where I put a receipt, and then I’m not even on the same subject anymore.\n\nSo… often? I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m walking around feeling guilty all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in almost all activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike a lot, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—nothing professional, just sitting around with it—but I don’t really do any of that now. I’m not sure. I mean… slower how?

Some mornings it takes me a while to get going, but I don’t know if that’s my body being slow or just me standing there thinking about what I’m supposed to do first. Like I’ll be by the kitchen counter and notice the mail stacked up, and then I’m thinking about the post office hours, and whether they changed them since that construction on the main road… and then I still haven’t put my shoes on.

Or I don’t even get far enough to find out, I guess.\n\nLike I stopped hiking, stopped coaching, stopped picking up the guitar. I don’t call people back. I’ll sit in that small rental place for hours, looking at stuff I know needs doing, thinking, “Okay, get up,” and then I just don’t. It’s weird because I used to be the guy who’d have everything lined up—gear in the truck, schedule set, all that—and now starting anything feels impossible. So yeah… I don’t know if it’s pleasure exactly, but I don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or stalled.\n\nSad sounds like crying or missing something specific, and sometimes I guess I do think about how I used to be, but then my mind goes off—like I’ll notice the trim on the wall needs fixing, and I’ll remember this old house my uncle had, and then an hour’s gone. So… I’m not sure. I haven’t really tracked it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been checking, honestly. I don’t have a scale at my place—well, I think there might’ve been one in the bathroom closet when I moved in, but I put a box in front of it and haven’t looked.\n\nMy clothes seem… maybe about the same? I mean, my belt’s always been a little weird because I punched an extra hole in it years ago, so that’s not a great measure. But no, I haven’t noticed some big weight drop or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, maybe.\n\nI’ll think I should’ve done something, or I’ll remember some little thing from work or from years ago, and it kind of sticks for a bit. But then it turns into something else, like I’ll start thinking about whether I locked a gate or where I put a receipt, and then I’m not even on the same subject anymore.\n\nSo… often? I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m walking around feeling guilty all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in almost all activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike a lot, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—nothing professional, just sitting around with it—but I don’t really do any of that now. Even calling people back, I just… don’t. I’ll see the phone and think I should, and then I’ll put it down, or I’ll start looking at something else, like the crack near the window in my rental, and then hours go by.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting there knowing there are things I’m supposed to do, things I used to want to do, and I can’t get myself started. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost interest in most of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since after I moved into the smaller rental. That was after the divorce, so… about a year and two months ago, maybe.\n\nIt’s hard to mark it exactly, because at first I thought it was just getting used to the place. The rooms are narrow, and the landlord painted over the hinges, which is strange because then the doors don’t close right—but yeah, that’s when things started unraveling, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's harder to make physical movements?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… slower how?\n\nSome mornings it takes me a while to get going, but I don’t know if that’s my body being slow or just me standing there thinking about what I’m supposed to do first. Like I’ll be by the kitchen counter and notice the mail stacked up, and then I’m thinking about the post office hours, and whether they changed them since that construction on the main road… and then I still haven’t put my shoes on.\n\nBut actual moving, like my arms or legs not working right? I don’t think I’ve noticed that exactly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"intent": "determine the presence of significant weight loss", "question": "Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit since you started feeling this way?"}

My clothes? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed, to be honest.

I mostly wear the same work pants and old shirts, and some of them were already kind of stretched out. The belt… I mean, sometimes I use a different hole, but I don’t keep track of that. I had one belt where the leather cracked near the buckle, but that was probably just old—my dad used to say you can tell a lot by the quality of leather, which is funny because he’d keep the worst shoes for mowing the lawn.

So… no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed a clear change in how they fit.

The rooms are narrow, and the landlord painted over the hinges, which is strange because then the doors don’t close right—but yeah, that’s when things started unraveling, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's harder to make physical movements?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… slower how?\n\nSome mornings it takes me a while to get going, but I don’t know if that’s my body being slow or just me standing there thinking about what I’m supposed to do first. Like I’ll be by the kitchen counter and notice the mail stacked up, and then I’m thinking about the post office hours, and whether they changed them since that construction on the main road… and then I still haven’t put my shoes on.\n\nBut actual moving, like my arms or legs not working right? I don’t think I’ve noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed, to be honest.\n\nI mostly wear the same work pants and old shirts, and some of them were already kind of stretched out. The belt… I mean, sometimes I use a different hole, but I don’t keep track of that. I had one belt where the leather cracked near the buckle, but that was probably just old—my dad used to say you can tell a lot by the quality of leather, which is funny because he’d keep the worst shoes for mowing the lawn.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed a clear change in how they fit.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Worthless… I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.

I get frustrated with myself, sure. Like if I leave something half-done or I can’t keep track of what I walked into a room for, then I’ll think, “Come on, what’s wrong with you?” But worthless, like… the whole person is no good? I don’t know if I’d say it that way.

People throw that word around too. At work, guys will say a piece of equipment is worthless when really it just needs the right part ordered, and then procurement takes three weeks because someone filled out the form wrong. So I guess I’m not sure what counts. I don’t sit there using that exact word much.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I used to do things all the time—hiking, coaching, playing guitar, calling people back. Now I don’t really do any of it.\n\nI sit in my little rental place for hours sometimes, knowing I should get up, clean, make a call, whatever, but I just… can’t start. It’s like the engine won’t turn over. I don’t know. I used to be dependable, you know? Protective services, schedules, radios, checklists—everything had a place. Now even answering a text feels like moving a couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you used to do, like hiking, coaching, or playing guitar?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to like hiking a lot, and coaching, and I’d pick up the guitar without thinking about it. Now it’s like those things are in another room with the door shut.\n\nI mostly sit in my rental place and think, “I should do something,” or “I should call someone back,” but I don’t. I’ll stare at the floor or the dishes or whatever for hours. It’s not even that I decided I don’t like them anymore… I just can’t seem to start. Like I’m not myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things that you never used to enjoy, or is it that nothing seems enjoyable to you anymore?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly nothing really feels enjoyable. Or I don’t even get far enough to find out, I guess.\n\nLike I stopped hiking, stopped coaching, stopped picking up the guitar. I don’t call people back. I’ll sit in that small rental place for hours, looking at stuff I know needs doing, thinking, “Okay, get up,” and then I just don’t. It’s weird because I used to be the guy who’d have everything lined up—gear in the truck, schedule set, all that—and now starting anything feels impossible. So yeah… I don’t know if it’s pleasure exactly, but I don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or stalled.\n\nSad sounds like crying or missing something specific, and sometimes I guess I do think about how I used to be, but then my mind goes off—like I’ll notice the trim on the wall needs fixing, and I’ll remember this old house my uncle had, and then an hour’s gone. So… I’m not sure. I haven’t really tracked it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been checking, honestly. I don’t have a scale at my place—well, I think there might’ve been one in the bathroom closet when I moved in, but I put a box in front of it and haven’t looked.\n\nMy clothes seem… maybe about the same? I mean, my belt’s always been a little weird because I punched an extra hole in it years ago, so that’s not a great measure. Not in a clear way, maybe.\n\nI’ll think I should’ve done something, or I’ll remember some little thing from work or from years ago, and it kind of sticks for a bit. But then it turns into something else, like I’ll start thinking about whether I locked a gate or where I put a receipt, and then I’m not even on the same subject anymore.\n\nSo… often? I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m walking around feeling guilty all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in almost all activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike a lot, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—nothing professional, just sitting around with it—but I don’t really do any of that now. Even calling people back, I just… don’t. I’ll see the phone and think I should, and then I’ll put it down, or I’ll start looking at something else, like the crack near the window in my rental, and then hours go by.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting there knowing there are things I’m supposed to do, things I used to want to do, and I can’t get myself started. Death? I mean… not in a planned-out way, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there making lists or anything.

It comes up, I guess, because with my job you hear about accidents and calls and people doing stupid things on icy roads, and then you start thinking how quick things can change. Like there was this one intersection near the old grocery store, they put in a new light but the timing is all wrong, and everybody knows it except the city, apparently. So I’ll think about stuff like that.

But do I often think about death… I don’t know. Sometimes the idea passes through, but I wouldn’t say I’m focused on it all the time.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I used to do things all the time—hiking, coaching, playing guitar, calling people back. Now I don’t really do any of it.\n\nI sit in my little rental place for hours sometimes, knowing I should get up, clean, make a call, whatever, but I just… can’t start. It’s like the engine won’t turn over. I don’t know. I used to be dependable, you know? Protective services, schedules, radios, checklists—everything had a place. Now even answering a text feels like moving a couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you used to do, like hiking, coaching, or playing guitar?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to like hiking a lot, and coaching, and I’d pick up the guitar without thinking about it. Now it’s like those things are in another room with the door shut.\n\nI mostly sit in my rental place and think, “I should do something,” or “I should call someone back,” but I don’t. I’ll stare at the floor or the dishes or whatever for hours. It’s not even that I decided I don’t like them anymore… I just can’t seem to start. Like I’m not myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things that you never used to enjoy, or is it that nothing seems enjoyable to you anymore?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly nothing really feels enjoyable. Or I don’t even get far enough to find out, I guess.\n\nLike I stopped hiking, stopped coaching, stopped picking up the guitar. I don’t call people back. I’ll sit in that small rental place for hours, looking at stuff I know needs doing, thinking, “Okay, get up,” and then I just don’t. It’s weird because I used to be the guy who’d have everything lined up—gear in the truck, schedule set, all that—and now starting anything feels impossible. So yeah… I don’t know if it’s pleasure exactly, but I don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or stalled.\n\nSad sounds like crying or missing something specific, and sometimes I guess I do think about how I used to be, but then my mind goes off—like I’ll notice the trim on the wall needs fixing, and I’ll remember this old house my uncle had, and then an hour’s gone. So… I’m not sure. I haven’t really tracked it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been checking, honestly. I don’t have a scale at my place—well, I think there might’ve been one in the bathroom closet when I moved in, but I put a box in front of it and haven’t looked.\n\nMy clothes seem… maybe about the same? I mean, my belt’s always been a little weird because I punched an extra hole in it years ago, so that’s not a great measure. But no, I haven’t noticed some big weight drop or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, maybe.\n\nI’ll think I should’ve done something, or I’ll remember some little thing from work or from years ago, and it kind of sticks for a bit. But then it turns into something else, like I’ll start thinking about whether I locked a gate or where I put a receipt, and then I’m not even on the same subject anymore.\n\nSo… often? I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m walking around feeling guilty all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in almost all activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike a lot, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—nothing professional, just sitting around with it—but I don’t really do any of that now. Even calling people back, I just… don’t. I’ll see the phone and think I should, and then I’ll put it down, or I’ll start looking at something else, like the crack near the window in my rental, and then hours go by.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting there knowing there are things I’m supposed to do, things I used to want to do, and I can’t get myself started. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost interest in most of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since after I moved into the smaller rental. That was after the divorce, so… about a year and two months ago, maybe.\n\nIt’s hard to mark it exactly, because at first I thought it was just getting used to the place. The rooms are narrow, and the landlord painted over the hinges, which is strange because then the doors don’t close right—but yeah, that’s when things started unraveling, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's harder to make physical movements?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… slower how?\n\nSome mornings it takes me a while to get going, but I don’t know if that’s my body being slow or just me standing there thinking about what I’m supposed to do first. Like I’ll be by the kitchen counter and notice the mail stacked up, and then I’m thinking about the post office hours, and whether they changed them since that construction on the main road… and then I still haven’t put my shoes on.\n\nBut actual moving, like my arms or legs not working right? I don’t think I’ve noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um… I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.\n\nI get frustrated with myself, sure. Like if I leave something half-done or I can’t keep track of what I walked into a room for, then I’ll think, “Come on, what’s wrong with you?” But worthless, like… the whole person is no good? Significant weight loss… I don’t know. I haven’t weighed myself regular, so I couldn’t tell you a number.

Like, the scale at home is in the bathroom closet behind a bunch of towels, and I only pull it out if someone mentions it, which… I mean, my brother got really into counting steps for a while and bought one of those watches, but then he stopped wearing it because it irritated his wrist. So I’m not really that kind of person, tracking things.

My clothes don’t feel dramatically different, I guess. Maybe up or down a little, but nothing where I thought, “Oh, I’m losing a lot of weight.” So… no, not that I’ve noticed.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I used to do things all the time—hiking, coaching, playing guitar, calling people back. Now I don’t really do any of it.\n\nI sit in my little rental place for hours sometimes, knowing I should get up, clean, make a call, whatever, but I just… can’t start. It’s like the engine won’t turn over. I don’t know. I used to be dependable, you know? Protective services, schedules, radios, checklists—everything had a place. Now even answering a text feels like moving a couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you used to do, like hiking, coaching, or playing guitar?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to like hiking a lot, and coaching, and I’d pick up the guitar without thinking about it. Now it’s like those things are in another room with the door shut.\n\nI mostly sit in my rental place and think, “I should do something,” or “I should call someone back,” but I don’t. I’ll stare at the floor or the dishes or whatever for hours. It’s not even that I decided I don’t like them anymore… I just can’t seem to start. Like I’m not myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things that you never used to enjoy, or is it that nothing seems enjoyable to you anymore?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly nothing really feels enjoyable. Or I don’t even get far enough to find out, I guess.\n\nLike I stopped hiking, stopped coaching, stopped picking up the guitar. I don’t call people back. I’ll sit in that small rental place for hours, looking at stuff I know needs doing, thinking, “Okay, get up,” and then I just don’t. It’s weird because I used to be the guy who’d have everything lined up—gear in the truck, schedule set, all that—and now starting anything feels impossible. So yeah… I don’t know if it’s pleasure exactly, but I don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or stalled.\n\nSad sounds like crying or missing something specific, and sometimes I guess I do think about how I used to be, but then my mind goes off—like I’ll notice the trim on the wall needs fixing, and I’ll remember this old house my uncle had, and then an hour’s gone. So… I’m not sure. I haven’t really tracked it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been checking, honestly. I don’t have a scale at my place—well, I think there might’ve been one in the bathroom closet when I moved in, but I put a box in front of it and haven’t looked.\n\nMy clothes seem… maybe about the same? I mean, my belt’s always been a little weird because I punched an extra hole in it years ago, so that’s not a great measure. But no, I haven’t noticed some big weight drop or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, maybe.\n\nI’ll think I should’ve done something, or I’ll remember some little thing from work or from years ago, and it kind of sticks for a bit. But then it turns into something else, like I’ll start thinking about whether I locked a gate or where I put a receipt, and then I’m not even on the same subject anymore.\n\nSo… often? I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m walking around feeling guilty all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in almost all activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike a lot, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—nothing professional, just sitting around with it—but I don’t really do any of that now. Even calling people back, I just… don’t. I’ll see the phone and think I should, and then I’ll put it down, or I’ll start looking at something else, like the crack near the window in my rental, and then hours go by.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting there knowing there are things I’m supposed to do, things I used to want to do, and I can’t get myself started. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost interest in most of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since after I moved into the smaller rental. That was after the divorce, so… about a year and two months ago, maybe.\n\nIt’s hard to mark it exactly, because at first I thought it was just getting used to the place. The rooms are narrow, and the landlord painted over the hinges, which is strange because then the doors don’t close right—but yeah, that’s when things started unraveling, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's harder to make physical movements?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… slower how?\n\nSome mornings it takes me a while to get going, but I don’t know if that’s my body being slow or just me standing there thinking about what I’m supposed to do first. Like I’ll be by the kitchen counter and notice the mail stacked up, and then I’m thinking about the post office hours, and whether they changed them since that construction on the main road… and then I still haven’t put my shoes on.\n\nBut actual moving, like my arms or legs not working right? I don’t think I’ve noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed, to be honest.\n\nI mostly wear the same work pants and old shirts, and some of them were already kind of stretched out. The belt… I mean, sometimes I use a different hole, but I don’t keep track of that. I had one belt where the leather cracked near the buckle, but that was probably just old—my dad used to say you can tell a lot by the quality of leather, which is funny because he’d keep the worst shoes for mowing the lawn.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed a clear change in how they fit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.\n\nI get frustrated with myself, sure. Like if I leave something half-done or I can’t keep track of what I walked into a room for, then I’ll think, “Come on, what’s wrong with you?” But worthless, like… the whole person is no good? I don’t know if I’d say it that way.\n\nPeople throw that word around too. At work, guys will say a piece of equipment is worthless when really it just needs the right part ordered, and then procurement takes three weeks because someone filled out the form wrong. I haven’t weighed myself regular, so I couldn’t tell you a number.\n\nLike, the scale at home is in the bathroom closet behind a bunch of towels, and I only pull it out if someone mentions it, which… I mean, my brother got really into counting steps for a while and bought one of those watches, but then he stopped wearing it because it irritated his wrist. So I’m not really that kind of person, tracking things.\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different, I guess. I don’t sit there making lists or anything.\n\nIt comes up, I guess, because with my job you hear about accidents and calls and people doing stupid things on icy roads, and then you start thinking how quick things can change. Like there was this one intersection near the old grocery store, they put in a new light but the timing is all wrong, and everybody knows it except the city, apparently. Harming myself… I don’t know. I mean, everybody says things like, “I could just disappear for a week,” when work gets stupid, but that’s not the same thing, right?

I’ve had rough days where I don’t like where my head is at, and I’ll just sit there too long, kind of staring. But actually harming myself… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I used to do things all the time—hiking, coaching, playing guitar, calling people back. Now I don’t really do any of it.\n\nI sit in my little rental place for hours sometimes, knowing I should get up, clean, make a call, whatever, but I just… can’t start. It’s like the engine won’t turn over. I don’t know. I used to be dependable, you know? Protective services, schedules, radios, checklists—everything had a place. Now even answering a text feels like moving a couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you used to do, like hiking, coaching, or playing guitar?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to like hiking a lot, and coaching, and I’d pick up the guitar without thinking about it. Now it’s like those things are in another room with the door shut.\n\nI mostly sit in my rental place and think, “I should do something,” or “I should call someone back,” but I don’t. I’ll stare at the floor or the dishes or whatever for hours. It’s not even that I decided I don’t like them anymore… I just can’t seem to start. Like I’m not myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things that you never used to enjoy, or is it that nothing seems enjoyable to you anymore?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly nothing really feels enjoyable. Or I don’t even get far enough to find out, I guess.\n\nLike I stopped hiking, stopped coaching, stopped picking up the guitar. I don’t call people back. I’ll sit in that small rental place for hours, looking at stuff I know needs doing, thinking, “Okay, get up,” and then I just don’t. It’s weird because I used to be the guy who’d have everything lined up—gear in the truck, schedule set, all that—and now starting anything feels impossible. So yeah… I don’t know if it’s pleasure exactly, but I don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or stalled.\n\nSad sounds like crying or missing something specific, and sometimes I guess I do think about how I used to be, but then my mind goes off—like I’ll notice the trim on the wall needs fixing, and I’ll remember this old house my uncle had, and then an hour’s gone. So… I’m not sure. I haven’t really tracked it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been checking, honestly. I don’t have a scale at my place—well, I think there might’ve been one in the bathroom closet when I moved in, but I put a box in front of it and haven’t looked.\n\nMy clothes seem… maybe about the same? I mean, my belt’s always been a little weird because I punched an extra hole in it years ago, so that’s not a great measure. But no, I haven’t noticed some big weight drop or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, maybe.\n\nI’ll think I should’ve done something, or I’ll remember some little thing from work or from years ago, and it kind of sticks for a bit. But then it turns into something else, like I’ll start thinking about whether I locked a gate or where I put a receipt, and then I’m not even on the same subject anymore.\n\nSo… often? I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m walking around feeling guilty all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in almost all activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike a lot, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—nothing professional, just sitting around with it—but I don’t really do any of that now. Even calling people back, I just… don’t. I’ll see the phone and think I should, and then I’ll put it down, or I’ll start looking at something else, like the crack near the window in my rental, and then hours go by.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting there knowing there are things I’m supposed to do, things I used to want to do, and I can’t get myself started. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost interest in most of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since after I moved into the smaller rental. That was after the divorce, so… about a year and two months ago, maybe.\n\nIt’s hard to mark it exactly, because at first I thought it was just getting used to the place. The rooms are narrow, and the landlord painted over the hinges, which is strange because then the doors don’t close right—but yeah, that’s when things started unraveling, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's harder to make physical movements?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… slower how?\n\nSome mornings it takes me a while to get going, but I don’t know if that’s my body being slow or just me standing there thinking about what I’m supposed to do first. Like I’ll be by the kitchen counter and notice the mail stacked up, and then I’m thinking about the post office hours, and whether they changed them since that construction on the main road… and then I still haven’t put my shoes on.\n\nBut actual moving, like my arms or legs not working right? I don’t think I’ve noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed, to be honest.\n\nI mostly wear the same work pants and old shirts, and some of them were already kind of stretched out. The belt… I mean, sometimes I use a different hole, but I don’t keep track of that. I had one belt where the leather cracked near the buckle, but that was probably just old—my dad used to say you can tell a lot by the quality of leather, which is funny because he’d keep the worst shoes for mowing the lawn.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed a clear change in how they fit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.\n\nI get frustrated with myself, sure. Like if I leave something half-done or I can’t keep track of what I walked into a room for, then I’ll think, “Come on, what’s wrong with you?” But worthless, like… the whole person is no good? I don’t know if I’d say it that way.\n\nPeople throw that word around too. At work, guys will say a piece of equipment is worthless when really it just needs the right part ordered, and then procurement takes three weeks because someone filled out the form wrong. I mean… not in a planned-out way, if that’s what you mean. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost interest in most of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since after I moved into the smaller rental. That was after the divorce, so… about a year and two months ago, maybe.\n\nIt’s hard to mark it exactly, because at first I thought it was just getting used to the place. The rooms are narrow, and the landlord painted over the hinges, which is strange because then the doors don’t close right—but yeah, that’s when things started unraveling, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's harder to make physical movements?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… slower how?\n\nSome mornings it takes me a while to get going, but I don’t know if that’s my body being slow or just me standing there thinking about what I’m supposed to do first. Like I’ll be by the kitchen counter and notice the mail stacked up, and then I’m thinking about the post office hours, and whether they changed them since that construction on the main road… and then I still haven’t put my shoes on.\n\nBut actual moving, like my arms or legs not working right? I don’t think I’ve noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed, to be honest.\n\nI mostly wear the same work pants and old shirts, and some of them were already kind of stretched out. The belt… I mean, sometimes I use a different hole, but I don’t keep track of that. I had one belt where the leather cracked near the buckle, but that was probably just old—my dad used to say you can tell a lot by the quality of leather, which is funny because he’d keep the worst shoes for mowing the lawn.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed a clear change in how they fit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.\n\nI get frustrated with myself, sure. Like if I leave something half-done or I can’t keep track of what I walked into a room for, then I’ll think, “Come on, what’s wrong with you?” But worthless, like… the whole person is no good? I don’t know if I’d say it that way.\n\nPeople throw that word around too. At work, guys will say a piece of equipment is worthless when really it just needs the right part ordered, and then procurement takes three weeks because someone filled out the form wrong. So I guess I’m not sure what counts. I don’t sit there using that exact word much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death?\n\nPatient: Death? I mean… not in a planned-out way, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there making lists or anything.\n\nIt comes up, I guess, because with my job you hear about accidents and calls and people doing stupid things on icy roads, and then you start thinking how quick things can change. Like there was this one intersection near the old grocery store, they put in a new light but the timing is all wrong, and everybody knows it except the city, apparently. So I’ll think about stuff like that.\n\nBut do I often think about death… I don’t know. Sometimes the idea passes through, but I wouldn’t say I’m focused on it all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss since you first started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Significant weight loss… I don’t know. I haven’t weighed myself regular, so I couldn’t tell you a number.\n\nLike, the scale at home is in the bathroom closet behind a bunch of towels, and I only pull it out if someone mentions it, which… I mean, my brother got really into counting steps for a while and bought one of those watches, but then he stopped wearing it because it irritated his wrist. So I’m not really that kind of person, tracking things.\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different, I guess. Maybe up or down a little, but nothing where I thought, “Oh, I’m losing a lot of weight.” So… no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Harming myself… I don’t know. I mean, everybody says things like, “I could just disappear for a week,” when work gets stupid, but that’s not the same thing, right?\n\nI’ve had rough days where I don’t like where my head is at, and I’ll just sit there too long, kind of staring. But actually harming myself… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I don’t have some clear thing to tell you. It’s more like the question gets big and then I start thinking about what counts as “harm,” like not sleeping enough, or skipping meals, or when guys at work pretend they’re fine and then they end up making dumb decisions.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I haven’t really put it into words like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. A burden… I mean, I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.

Sometimes I feel like people probably get tired of me, or like I’m taking up space when everyone’s got their own stuff going on. But then I think, well, everybody needs help sometimes, right? Like at work, if one person messes up the schedule, it throws everybody off, and then suddenly you’re calling three different people just to cover one shift, and nobody says “burden,” they just say the schedule’s a mess.

So… I don’t know if I feel like a burden exactly. Maybe sometimes I worry I’m making things harder for people, but I’m not sure.

Now I don’t really do any of it.\n\nI sit in my little rental place for hours sometimes, knowing I should get up, clean, make a call, whatever, but I just… can’t start. It’s like the engine won’t turn over. I don’t know. I used to be dependable, you know? Protective services, schedules, radios, checklists—everything had a place. Now even answering a text feels like moving a couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you used to do, like hiking, coaching, or playing guitar?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to like hiking a lot, and coaching, and I’d pick up the guitar without thinking about it. Now it’s like those things are in another room with the door shut.\n\nI mostly sit in my rental place and think, “I should do something,” or “I should call someone back,” but I don’t. I’ll stare at the floor or the dishes or whatever for hours. It’s not even that I decided I don’t like them anymore… I just can’t seem to start. Like I’m not myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things that you never used to enjoy, or is it that nothing seems enjoyable to you anymore?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly nothing really feels enjoyable. Or I don’t even get far enough to find out, I guess.\n\nLike I stopped hiking, stopped coaching, stopped picking up the guitar. I don’t call people back. I’ll sit in that small rental place for hours, looking at stuff I know needs doing, thinking, “Okay, get up,” and then I just don’t. It’s weird because I used to be the guy who’d have everything lined up—gear in the truck, schedule set, all that—and now starting anything feels impossible. So yeah… I don’t know if it’s pleasure exactly, but I don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or stalled.\n\nSad sounds like crying or missing something specific, and sometimes I guess I do think about how I used to be, but then my mind goes off—like I’ll notice the trim on the wall needs fixing, and I’ll remember this old house my uncle had, and then an hour’s gone. So… I’m not sure. I haven’t really tracked it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been checking, honestly. I don’t have a scale at my place—well, I think there might’ve been one in the bathroom closet when I moved in, but I put a box in front of it and haven’t looked.\n\nMy clothes seem… maybe about the same? I mean, my belt’s always been a little weird because I punched an extra hole in it years ago, so that’s not a great measure. But no, I haven’t noticed some big weight drop or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, maybe.\n\nI’ll think I should’ve done something, or I’ll remember some little thing from work or from years ago, and it kind of sticks for a bit. But then it turns into something else, like I’ll start thinking about whether I locked a gate or where I put a receipt, and then I’m not even on the same subject anymore.\n\nSo… often? I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m walking around feeling guilty all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in almost all activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike a lot, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—nothing professional, just sitting around with it—but I don’t really do any of that now. Even calling people back, I just… don’t. I’ll see the phone and think I should, and then I’ll put it down, or I’ll start looking at something else, like the crack near the window in my rental, and then hours go by.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting there knowing there are things I’m supposed to do, things I used to want to do, and I can’t get myself started. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost interest in most of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since after I moved into the smaller rental. That was after the divorce, so… about a year and two months ago, maybe.\n\nIt’s hard to mark it exactly, because at first I thought it was just getting used to the place. The rooms are narrow, and the landlord painted over the hinges, which is strange because then the doors don’t close right—but yeah, that’s when things started unraveling, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's harder to make physical movements?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… slower how?\n\nSome mornings it takes me a while to get going, but I don’t know if that’s my body being slow or just me standing there thinking about what I’m supposed to do first. Like I’ll be by the kitchen counter and notice the mail stacked up, and then I’m thinking about the post office hours, and whether they changed them since that construction on the main road… and then I still haven’t put my shoes on.\n\nBut actual moving, like my arms or legs not working right? I don’t think I’ve noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed, to be honest.\n\nI mostly wear the same work pants and old shirts, and some of them were already kind of stretched out. The belt… I mean, sometimes I use a different hole, but I don’t keep track of that. I had one belt where the leather cracked near the buckle, but that was probably just old—my dad used to say you can tell a lot by the quality of leather, which is funny because he’d keep the worst shoes for mowing the lawn.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed a clear change in how they fit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.\n\nI get frustrated with myself, sure. Like if I leave something half-done or I can’t keep track of what I walked into a room for, then I’ll think, “Come on, what’s wrong with you?” But worthless, like… the whole person is no good? I don’t know if I’d say it that way.\n\nPeople throw that word around too. At work, guys will say a piece of equipment is worthless when really it just needs the right part ordered, and then procurement takes three weeks because someone filled out the form wrong. So I guess I’m not sure what counts. I don’t sit there using that exact word much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death?\n\nPatient: Death? I mean… not in a planned-out way, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there making lists or anything.\n\nIt comes up, I guess, because with my job you hear about accidents and calls and people doing stupid things on icy roads, and then you start thinking how quick things can change. Like there was this one intersection near the old grocery store, they put in a new light but the timing is all wrong, and everybody knows it except the city, apparently. So I’ll think about stuff like that.\n\nBut do I often think about death… I don’t know. Sometimes the idea passes through, but I wouldn’t say I’m focused on it all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss since you first started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Significant weight loss… I don’t know. I haven’t weighed myself regular, so I couldn’t tell you a number.\n\nLike, the scale at home is in the bathroom closet behind a bunch of towels, and I only pull it out if someone mentions it, which… I mean, my brother got really into counting steps for a while and bought one of those watches, but then he stopped wearing it because it irritated his wrist. So I’m not really that kind of person, tracking things.\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different, I guess. Maybe up or down a little, but nothing where I thought, “Oh, I’m losing a lot of weight.” So… no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Harming myself… I don’t know. I mean, everybody says things like, “I could just disappear for a week,” when work gets stupid, but that’s not the same thing, right?\n\nI’ve had rough days where I don’t like where my head is at, and I’ll just sit there too long, kind of staring. But actually harming myself… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I don’t have some clear thing to tell you. It’s more like the question gets big and then I start thinking about what counts as “harm,” like not sleeping enough, or skipping meals, or when guys at work pretend they’re fine and then they end up making dumb decisions.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I haven’t really put it into words like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a burden to others?\n\nPatient: A burden… I mean, I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.\n\nSometimes I feel like people probably get tired of me, or like I’m taking up space when everyone’s got their own stuff going on. But then I think, well, everybody needs help sometimes, right? Like at work, if one person messes up the schedule, it throws everybody off, and then suddenly you’re calling three different people just to cover one shift, and nobody says “burden,” they just say the schedule’s a mess.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I feel like a burden exactly. Maybe sometimes I worry I’m making things harder for people, but I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I mean, most of the time, yes, probably.

It’s not just like having a bad week and then you get back into your routine. I was suspended from work — protective service work — after this routine patrol where I… I scared some staff and people standing around. It wasn’t supposed to be anything, just normal rounds, and then suddenly everyone’s looking at me like I’m the problem.

And since then, things kind of collapsed outward. I used to coach a bit, volunteer, people would call me because they knew I’d show up. Now I don’t really do much of that. I’ve pulled back from most people. It’s easier not to explain, because once you start explaining, they ask more questions, and then you’re standing there in a grocery aisle talking about your whole life when all you wanted was cereal.

So yes… it’s interfering. More than I like to admit.

I had one belt where the leather cracked near the buckle, but that was probably just old—my dad used to say you can tell a lot by the quality of leather, which is funny because he’d keep the worst shoes for mowing the lawn.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed a clear change in how they fit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.\n\nI get frustrated with myself, sure. Like if I leave something half-done or I can’t keep track of what I walked into a room for, then I’ll think, “Come on, what’s wrong with you?” But worthless, like… the whole person is no good? I don’t know if I’d say it that way.\n\nPeople throw that word around too. At work, guys will say a piece of equipment is worthless when really it just needs the right part ordered, and then procurement takes three weeks because someone filled out the form wrong. So I guess I’m not sure what counts. I don’t sit there using that exact word much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death?\n\nPatient: Death? I mean… not in a planned-out way, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there making lists or anything.\n\nIt comes up, I guess, because with my job you hear about accidents and calls and people doing stupid things on icy roads, and then you start thinking how quick things can change. Like there was this one intersection near the old grocery store, they put in a new light but the timing is all wrong, and everybody knows it except the city, apparently. So I’ll think about stuff like that.\n\nBut do I often think about death… I don’t know. Sometimes the idea passes through, but I wouldn’t say I’m focused on it all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss since you first started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Significant weight loss… I don’t know. I haven’t weighed myself regular, so I couldn’t tell you a number.\n\nLike, the scale at home is in the bathroom closet behind a bunch of towels, and I only pull it out if someone mentions it, which… I mean, my brother got really into counting steps for a while and bought one of those watches, but then he stopped wearing it because it irritated his wrist. So I’m not really that kind of person, tracking things.\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different, I guess. Maybe up or down a little, but nothing where I thought, “Oh, I’m losing a lot of weight.” So… no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Harming myself… I don’t know. I mean, everybody says things like, “I could just disappear for a week,” when work gets stupid, but that’s not the same thing, right?\n\nI’ve had rough days where I don’t like where my head is at, and I’ll just sit there too long, kind of staring. But actually harming myself… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I don’t have some clear thing to tell you. It’s more like the question gets big and then I start thinking about what counts as “harm,” like not sleeping enough, or skipping meals, or when guys at work pretend they’re fine and then they end up making dumb decisions.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I haven’t really put it into words like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a burden to others?\n\nPatient: A burden… I mean, I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.\n\nSometimes I feel like people probably get tired of me, or like I’m taking up space when everyone’s got their own stuff going on. But then I think, well, everybody needs help sometimes, right? Like at work, if one person messes up the schedule, it throws everybody off, and then suddenly you’re calling three different people just to cover one shift, and nobody says “burden,” they just say the schedule’s a mess.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I feel like a burden exactly. Maybe sometimes I worry I’m making things harder for people, but I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current state is interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, most of the time, yes, probably.\n\nIt’s not just like having a bad week and then you get back into your routine. I was suspended from work — protective service work — after this routine patrol where I… I scared some staff and people standing around. It wasn’t supposed to be anything, just normal rounds, and then suddenly everyone’s looking at me like I’m the problem.\n\nAnd since then, things kind of collapsed outward. I used to coach a bit, volunteer, people would call me because they knew I’d show up. Now I don’t really do much of that. I’ve pulled back from most people. It’s easier not to explain, because once you start explaining, they ask more questions, and then you’re standing there in a grocery aisle talking about your whole life when all you wanted was cereal.\n\nSo yes… it’s interfering. More than I like to admit.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Yeah… most days, I think. It’s not like I wake up and decide, “I’m not interested in anything today.” It’s more like everything just sort of… doesn’t reach me.

I used to hike, coach, play guitar a little, call people back when they left messages. I had things. Now I’ll be in that small rental place and I’ll know there are things I should do, even simple things, and I just sit there for hours. Like I’m waiting for the engine to turn over, but it doesn’t.

Some days I tell myself it’s just being tired, or it’s the weather, or everybody gets in a rut. My old hiking boots are still by the door, actually, which is stupid because I’m not using them, and they get in the way when I bring groceries in. But moving them feels like admitting something.

So… yeah. It’s there pretty much every day.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I used to do things all the time—hiking, coaching, playing guitar, calling people back. Now I don’t really do any of it.\n\nI sit in my little rental place for hours sometimes, knowing I should get up, clean, make a call, whatever, but I just… can’t start. It’s like the engine won’t turn over. I don’t know. I used to be dependable, you know? Protective services, schedules, radios, checklists—everything had a place. Now even answering a text feels like moving a couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you used to do, like hiking, coaching, or playing guitar?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to like hiking a lot, and coaching, and I’d pick up the guitar without thinking about it. Now it’s like those things are in another room with the door shut.\n\nI mostly sit in my rental place and think, “I should do something,” or “I should call someone back,” but I don’t. I’ll stare at the floor or the dishes or whatever for hours. It’s not even that I decided I don’t like them anymore… I just can’t seem to start. Like I’m not myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things that you never used to enjoy, or is it that nothing seems enjoyable to you anymore?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly nothing really feels enjoyable. Or I don’t even get far enough to find out, I guess.\n\nLike I stopped hiking, stopped coaching, stopped picking up the guitar. I don’t call people back. I’ll sit in that small rental place for hours, looking at stuff I know needs doing, thinking, “Okay, get up,” and then I just don’t. It’s weird because I used to be the guy who’d have everything lined up—gear in the truck, schedule set, all that—and now starting anything feels impossible. So yeah… I don’t know if it’s pleasure exactly, but I don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or stalled.\n\nSad sounds like crying or missing something specific, and sometimes I guess I do think about how I used to be, but then my mind goes off—like I’ll notice the trim on the wall needs fixing, and I’ll remember this old house my uncle had, and then an hour’s gone. So… I’m not sure. I haven’t really tracked it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been checking, honestly. I don’t have a scale at my place—well, I think there might’ve been one in the bathroom closet when I moved in, but I put a box in front of it and haven’t looked.\n\nMy clothes seem… maybe about the same? I mean, my belt’s always been a little weird because I punched an extra hole in it years ago, so that’s not a great measure. But no, I haven’t noticed some big weight drop or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, maybe.\n\nI’ll think I should’ve done something, or I’ll remember some little thing from work or from years ago, and it kind of sticks for a bit. But then it turns into something else, like I’ll start thinking about whether I locked a gate or where I put a receipt, and then I’m not even on the same subject anymore.\n\nSo… often? I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m walking around feeling guilty all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in almost all activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike a lot, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—nothing professional, just sitting around with it—but I don’t really do any of that now. Even calling people back, I just… don’t. I’ll see the phone and think I should, and then I’ll put it down, or I’ll start looking at something else, like the crack near the window in my rental, and then hours go by.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting there knowing there are things I’m supposed to do, things I used to want to do, and I can’t get myself started. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost interest in most of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since after I moved into the smaller rental. That was after the divorce, so… about a year and two months ago, maybe.\n\nIt’s hard to mark it exactly, because at first I thought it was just getting used to the place. The rooms are narrow, and the landlord painted over the hinges, which is strange because then the doors don’t close right—but yeah, that’s when things started unraveling, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's harder to make physical movements?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… slower how?\n\nSome mornings it takes me a while to get going, but I don’t know if that’s my body being slow or just me standing there thinking about what I’m supposed to do first. Like I’ll be by the kitchen counter and notice the mail stacked up, and then I’m thinking about the post office hours, and whether they changed them since that construction on the main road… and then I still haven’t put my shoes on.\n\nBut actual moving, like my arms or legs not working right? I don’t think I’ve noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed, to be honest.\n\nI mostly wear the same work pants and old shirts, and some of them were already kind of stretched out. The belt… I mean, sometimes I use a different hole, but I don’t keep track of that. I had one belt where the leather cracked near the buckle, but that was probably just old—my dad used to say you can tell a lot by the quality of leather, which is funny because he’d keep the worst shoes for mowing the lawn.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed a clear change in how they fit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… I don’t know. That’s a heavy word.\n\nI get frustrated with myself, sure. Like if I leave something half-done or I can’t keep track of what I walked into a room for, then I’ll think, “Come on, what’s wrong with you?” But worthless, like… the whole person is no good? I don’t know if I’d say it that way.\n\nPeople throw that word around too. At work, guys will say a piece of equipment is worthless when really it just needs the right part ordered, and then procurement takes three weeks because someone filled out the form wrong. So I guess I’m not sure what counts. I don’t sit there using that exact word much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death?\n\nPatient: Death? I mean… not in a planned-out way, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there making lists or anything.\n\nIt comes up, I guess, because with my job you hear about accidents and calls and people doing stupid things on icy roads, and then you start thinking how quick things can change. Like there was this one intersection near the old grocery store, they put in a new light but the timing is all wrong, and everybody knows it except the city, apparently. So I’ll think about stuff like that.\n\nBut do I often think about death… I don’t know. Sometimes the idea passes through, but I wouldn’t say I’m focused on it all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss since you first started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Significant weight loss… I don’t know. I haven’t weighed myself regular, so I couldn’t tell you a number.\n\nLike, the scale at home is in the bathroom closet behind a bunch of towels, and I only pull it out if someone mentions it, which… I mean, my brother got really into counting steps for a while and bought one of those watches, but then he stopped wearing it because it irritated his wrist. So I’m not really that kind of person, tracking things.\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different, I guess. Maybe up or down a little, but nothing where I thought, “Oh, I’m losing a lot of weight.” So… no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Harming myself… I don’t know. I mean, everybody says things like, “I could just disappear for a week,” when work gets stupid, but that’s not the same thing, right?\n\nI’ve had rough days where I don’t like where my head is at, and I’ll just sit there too long, kind of staring. But actually harming myself… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I don’t have some clear thing to tell you. It’s more like the question gets big and then I start thinking about what counts as “harm,” like not sleeping enough, or skipping meals, or when guys at work pretend they’re fine and then they end up making dumb decisions.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I haven’t really put it into words like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a burden to others?\n\nPatient: A burden… I mean, I don’t know. But then I think, well, everybody needs help sometimes, right? Like at work, if one person messes up the schedule, it throws everybody off, and then suddenly you’re calling three different people just to cover one shift, and nobody says “burden,” they just say the schedule’s a mess.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I feel like a burden exactly. Maybe sometimes I worry I’m making things harder for people, but I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current state is interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, most of the time, yes, probably.\n\nIt’s not just like having a bad week and then you get back into your routine. I was suspended from work — protective service work — after this routine patrol where I… I scared some staff and people standing around. I used to coach a bit, volunteer, people would call me because they knew I’d show up. Now I don’t really do much of that. I’ve pulled back from most people. It’s easier not to explain, because once you start explaining, they ask more questions, and then you’re standing there in a grocery aisle talking about your whole life when all you wanted was cereal.\n\nSo yes… it’s interfering. More than I like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities is present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. But then I think, well, everybody needs help sometimes, right? Like at work, if one person messes up the schedule, it throws everybody off, and then suddenly you’re calling three different people just to cover one shift, and nobody says “burden,” they just say the schedule’s a mess.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I feel like a burden exactly. Maybe sometimes I worry I’m making things harder for people, but I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current state is interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, most of the time, yes, probably.\n\nIt’s not just like having a bad week and then you get back into your routine. I was suspended from work — protective service work — after this routine patrol where I… I scared some staff and people standing around. It wasn’t supposed to be anything, just normal rounds, and then suddenly everyone’s looking at me like I’m the problem.\n\nAnd since then, things kind of collapsed outward. I used to coach a bit, volunteer, people would call me because they knew I’d show up. Now I don’t really do much of that. I’ve pulled back from most people. It’s easier not to explain, because once you start explaining, they ask more questions, and then you’re standing there in a grocery aisle talking about your whole life when all you wanted was cereal.\n\nSo yes… it’s interfering. More than I like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities is present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It’s not like I wake up and decide, “I’m not interested in anything today.” It’s more like everything just sort of… doesn’t reach me.\n\nI used to hike, coach, play guitar a little, call people back when they left messages. I had things. Now I’ll be in that small rental place and I’ll know there are things I should do, even simple things, and I just sit there for hours. Like I’m waiting for the engine to turn over, but it doesn’t.\n\nSome days I tell myself it’s just being tired, or it’s the weather, or everybody gets in a rut. My old hiking boots are still by the door, actually, which is stupid because I’m not using them, and they get in the way when I bring groceries in. But moving them feels like admitting something.\n\nSo… yeah. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Most of the time, yeah… I think that’s fair to say.

It’s not that nothing is ever nice, exactly. Like, coffee still tastes like coffee, and sometimes there’s a good song on somewhere — though I don’t really play guitar anymore, which is funny because I used to keep it right by the couch, and now it’s just kind of… there.

Mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I stopped hiking, stopped coaching, stopped calling people back. Some days I sit in that small rental for hours knowing there are things I should do, even easy things, and I just can’t get myself to start.

So pleasure… I don’t know. It’s like it’s supposed to be there, but it doesn’t land the way it used to.
